p=[o for o in g if o['type'] in('Method','Method of Reasoning')]

slots=[o['slot'] for o in p+n]

## PT102-S3-Q16 | Dispute | C

> Motorcoach driver: Professional drivers spend much more time driving, on average, than do other people and hence are more competent drivers than are other, less experienced drivers. Therefore, the speed limit on major highways should not be reduced, because that action would have the undesirable effect of forcing some people who are now both law-abiding and competent drivers to break the law.

Police officer: All drivers can drive within the legal speed limit if they wish, so it is not true to say that reducing the speed limit would be the cause of such illegal behavior.
>
> The point at issue between the motorcoach driver and the police officer is whether
>
> (A) it would be desirable to reduce the speed limit on major highways
> (B) professional drivers will drive within the legal speed limit if that limit is reduced
> (C) reducing the speed limit on major highways would cause some professional drivers to break the law
> (D) professional drivers are more competent drivers than are other, less experienced drivers
> (E) all drivers wish to drive within the speed limit

### Argument Breakdown

**Motorcoach driver's Position:** Lowering the speed limit would force some competent, law-abiding drivers to break the law, so it should not be lowered.

**Police officer's Position:** Every driver can stay under the limit if they want to, so a lower limit would not *cause* anyone to break the law.

**Evaluate:** The officer answers only one of the driver's claims: that a lower limit would *cause* lawbreaking. The officer says nothing about whether professional drivers are more competent, or whether lowering the limit is a good idea. The clash is on cause.

**Goal:** Whether lowering the speed limit would cause some drivers to break the law.

### Choice Notes

**(A) Half Scope.** This is the most popular wrong answer. The driver clearly thinks lowering the limit is undesirable. But the officer never says whether it is a good idea. They only dispute the "cause" claim, so we cannot support the officer's side here.

**(B) Half Scope.** The officer says drivers *can* stay within the limit, not that they *will*. "Can" and "will" are different claims.

**(C) Correct.** The driver says a lower limit would force lawbreaking. The officer says it would not be the cause. That is the disagreement.

**(D) Half Scope.** The driver says professionals are more competent. The officer does not address competence.

**(E) Out of Scope.** Neither speaker talks about what all drivers *wish*.

"""Validate a batch of LR explanation drafts (usage: generate_batch_sql.py NN [bank_dir]) and emit guarded insert-only SQL, one transaction per slot. No database writes.

Handles four slot states:
  unloaded     question absent: insert question + choices (bank text) + explanation
  unexplained  question present, no single/standard explanation: insert explanation
  notes-only   explanation has steps but no notes: insert five notes
  partial      explanation has steps and some notes: insert only the missing letters
"""

import sys
from pathlib import Path
sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]))
from project_paths import project_path
import json
import re
from collections import defaultdict

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3]
BATCH = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "02"
DRAFTS = sorted((ROOT / "corpus/lr-explanations/drafts").glob(f"batch-{BATCH}-*-part-*.md"))
DB = ROOT / "system/scripts/lsatlab-pull/data/db"
BANK = Path(sys.argv[2]) if len(sys.argv) > 2 else project_path("lsatu-wt-bankfix-b02", "public", "data")
OUT = ROOT / f"corpus/lr-explanations/work/batch-{BATCH}-inputs"
Q = f"$batch{BATCH}$"
NOTE = f"Batch {BATCH}; question text from local bank."
TYPE_FIX = {"PT147-S4-Q16": "Parallel Flaw", "PT156-S4-Q24": "Parallel Flaw"}
TEXT_FIX = {("PT127-S1-Q4", "questionStem"): ("�", "’")}


def lit(s):
    if Q in s:
        raise ValueError("delimiter in text")
    return f"{Q}{s}{Q}"


def norm_text(s):
    """Collapse hard line wraps inside paragraphs; keep blank-line paragraph breaks."""
    paras = re.split(r"\n\s*\n", s.strip())
    return "\n\n".join(re.sub(r"\s*\n\s*", " ", p).strip() for p in paras)


def records(name):
    return json.loads((DB / f"{name}.json").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))


def parse_drafts():
    slots = {}
    for path in DRAFTS:
        text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
        for sec in text.split("\n## ")[1:]:
            head = sec.split("\n", 1)[0]
            slot, qtype, key = [x.strip() for x in head.split("|")]
            steps, notes = [], {}
            bd = sec.split("### Argument Breakdown", 1)
            if len(bd) == 2:
                part = bd[1].split("### Choice Notes", 1)[0]
                for m in re.finditer(r"^\*\*(.+?):\*\* (.+?)(?=\n\n\*\*|\Z)", part.strip(), re.S | re.M):
                    steps.append((m.group(1).strip(), m.group(2).strip()))
            cn = sec.split("### Choice Notes", 1)[1] if "### Choice Notes" in sec else ""
            for m in re.finditer(r"^\*\*\(([A-E])\) (.+?)\.\*\* (.+?)(?=\n\n\*\*\(|\Z)", cn.strip(), re.S | re.M):
                notes[m.group(1)] = (m.group(2).strip(), m.group(3).strip())
            slots[slot] = dict(type=qtype, key=key, steps=steps, notes=notes)
    return slots


def main():
    drafts = parse_drafts()
    bank = {}
    for f in BANK.glob("PT*-S*-LR.json"):
        for b in json.loads(f.read_text(encoding="utf-8")):
            bank[f"{b['practiceTest']}-S{b['sectionNumber']}-Q{b['questionNumber']}"] = b
    qs = {q["qid"]: q for q in records("lr_questions")}
    exq = defaultdict(list)
    for e in records("lr_explanations"):
        if e["style"] in ("single", "standard"):
            exq[e["question_id"]].append(e)
    st = defaultdict(list)
    for s in records("lr_explanation_steps"):
        st[s["explanation_id"]].append(s)
    nt = defaultdict(dict)
dict_keys(['slot', 'state', 'type', 'diff', 'feat', 'reason'])

## PT104-S1-Q7 | Paradox | C

**Modelled on:** PT148-S3-Q11 (difference between groups), PT140-S1-Q20 (part vs whole).


### Argument Breakdown

**Given that:** Most magazines have lost advertising revenue in the recession, enough to put the most widely read magazines in danger.

**How can it be that:** People are reading more magazines than ever, and more magazines are financially successful than ever?

**Evaluate:** Notice the two groups the stimulus names. The danger is to the *most widely read* magazines. The success is counted across magazines in general. If smaller magazines do not depend much on advertising, an ad slump would hurt the big ones while the rest thrive on more readers. Look for something that separates how the two kinds of magazine make money.

**Goal:** Find a difference in revenue source between the most widely read magazines and the others.

### Choice Notes

**(A) Half Scope.** Cheaper ad rates would explain the fall in ad revenue. They do nothing to explain why more magazines than ever are succeeding.

**(B) Out of Scope.** Television audiences do not tell us anything about magazine revenue or magazine success.

**(C) Correct.** Only the biggest magazines live on advertising, so the ad slump threatens them. Everyone else lives on circulation, and more people are reading, so more of them succeed. Both halves of the stimulus now fit.

**(D) Deepens Paradox.** If readers cut subscriptions and borrow copies, circulation revenue falls too. That makes the record number of successful magazines even harder to explain.

**(E) Restates Paradox.** New magazines surviving is one more sign of success. It repeats the surprising fact instead of explaining it.
[/external_agent_tool_result]

type RenderableLRQuestionInput = {
  stimulus?: unknown;
  questionStem?: unknown;
  answerChoices?: Partial<Record<'A' | 'B' | 'C' | 'D' | 'E', unknown>>;
};

const sharedPassageMetadataPrefixPattern =
  /^\s*(?:The following passage is used for Section\s+\d+,\s+questions\s+\d+\s+through\s+\d+\.|Questions?\s+\d+\s+(?:through|and|[-–])\s+\d+\s+refer to the following passage\.)\s*/i;
const sharedPassageRangePattern =
  /^\s*(?:The following passage is used for Section\s+\d+,\s+questions\s+(\d+)\s+through\s+(\d+)\.|Questions?\s+(\d+)\s+(?:through|and|[-–])\s+(\d+)\s+refer to the following passage\.)\s*/i;

function stripSharedPassageMetadataPrefix(text: string): string {
  return text.replace(sharedPassageMetadataPrefixPattern, '').trim();
}

function normalizeProseHardWraps(text: string): string {
  return text
    .replace(/\r\n?/g, '\n')
    .split(/\n{2,}/)
    .map((paragraph) => paragraph.replace(/[ \t]*\n[ \t]*/g, ' ').replace(/[ \t]{2,}/g, ' ').trim())
    .filter(Boolean)
    .join('\n\n');
}

function getSharedPassageRange(text: string): { start: number; end: number } | null {
  const match = text.match(sharedPassageRangePattern);
  if (!match) return null;

  const start = Number(match[1] ?? match[3]);
  const end = Number(match[2] ?? match[4]);
  if (!Number.isFinite(start) || !Number.isFinite(end) || start > end) return null;

  return { start, end };
}

const dialogueIntroPattern = /^[A-Z][A-Za-z’' -]{1,80}:\s+/;
const dialogueTransitionPattern = /[.!?]["”')\]]?\s+[A-Z][A-Za-z’' -]{1,80}:\s+/;
// Captures: 1) sentence-ending punctuation, 2) the candidate speaker label
// (without its trailing colon/space), 3) the colon + whitespace that follows.
const dialogueTurnBreakPattern =
  /([.!?]["”')\]]?)\s+([A-Z][A-Za-z’' -]{1,80})(:\s+)/g;

function normalizeDialogueSpeakerBreaks(text: string): string {
  if (!dialogueIntroPattern.test(text) || !dialogueTransitionPattern.test(text)) return text;

  return text.replace(dialogueTurnBreakPattern, (match, punct, label, colon) =>
    looksLikeSpeakerLabel(label) ? `${punct}\n\n${label}${colon}` : match,
  );
}

export function normalizeStimulusText(text: string): string {
  const sharedRange = getSharedPassageRange(text);
  let normalized = stripSharedPassageMetadataPrefix(text);

  if (sharedRange) {
    normalized = normalized
      .replace(new RegExp(`\\s+${sharedRange.start}\\.$`), '')
      .trim();

command: cd C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-wt-bankfix-b02 && git add public/data && git commit -q -m "Join hyphenated words split by hard wraps in the LR bank

The loader turns single newlines into spaces, so a compound hyphenated
at a line break rendered with a stray space (\"long- term\", \"30,000- year\").
Joins 18 such compounds across 10 files, and fixes \"one- fourth\" in
PT119-S2-Q17.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>" && git push -q -u origin fix/lr-bank-hyphen-wraps 2>&1 | grep -v "^remote" ; gh pr create --repo vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db --base main --head fix/lr-bank-hyphen-wraps --title "Join hyphenated words split by line wraps in the LR bank" --body "\`normalizeProseHardWraps\` in \`src/lib/questionLoader.ts\` turns a single newline into a space. When a hyphenated compound was wrapped at its hyphen in the source PDF, students saw a stray space: \"long- term\", \"30,000- year\", \"full- time\", \"cell- phone\".

- Joins 18 such compounds across 10 bank files (PT142, PT150, PT151, PT156, PT157, PT158).
- Fixes \"one- fourth\" in PT119-S2-Q17.

Matched only where a letter or digit comes before the hyphen and a lowercase letter follows the wrap. Suspended hyphens like \"low- and high-\" are left alone. Every edited file still parses as JSON.

     ```python
     SPEAKER = r"(?:(?:Mr|Ms|Mrs|Dr)\. )?[A-Z][A-Za-z]+(?: [A-Za-z]+)?: "
     def speaker_breaks(s):
         """In a dialogue stimulus, start each later speaker on its own line (matches the database convention)."""
         if not re.match(SPEAKER, s):
             return s
         def brk(m):
             return m.group(1) + (" " if m.group(1) in ("Mr.", "Ms.", "Mrs.", "Dr.") else "\n")
         return re.sub(r"(\S*[.?!\u201d\"])\s+(?=" + SPEAKER + ")", brk, s)
     ```

import json,glob,collections
R='C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/'
B='C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-wt-bankfix-b02/public/data/'
DB=R+'system/scripts/lsatlab-pull/data/db/'
L=lambda t: json.load(open(DB+t+'.json',encoding='utf-8'))
g=json.load(open(R+'corpus/lr-explanations/work/pilot-inputs/gaps.json'))
p=[o for o in g if o['type'] in('Most Supported','Most Strongly Supported')]
n=[o for o in g if o['type'] in('Main Conclusion',)][:3]
slots=[o['slot'] for o in p+n]
bank={}
for f in glob.glob(B+'PT*-S*-LR.json'):
  for b in json.load(open(f,encoding='utf-8')):
    bank[f"{b['practiceTest']}-S{b['sectionNumber']}-Q{b['questionNumber']}"]=b
Q={q['qid']:q for q in L('lr_questions')}
E=collections.defaultdict(list)
for e in L('lr_explanations'): E[e['question_id']].append(e)
S=collections.defaultdict(list)
for s in L('lr_explanation_steps'): S[s['explanation_id']].append(s)
N=collections.defaultdict(list)
for x in L('lr_explanation_choice_notes'): N[x['explanation_id']].append(x)
C=collections.defaultdict(list)
for c in L('lr_answer_choices'): C[c['question_id']].append(c)
for sl in slots:
  b=bank[sl]; print('=====',sl,b.get('questionType'),'KEY',b.get('correctAnswer'))

  print(b.get('stimulus')); print('Q:',b.get('questionStem') or b.get('prompt'))
  for k,v in b['answerChoices'].items(): print(' ',k,v)
  q=Q.get(sl)
  if q:
    for c in sorted(C[q['id']],key=lambda c:c['letter']): print('  DBtag',c['letter'],c['tag'],c.get('student_percent'))
    for e in E[q['id']]:
      for s in sorted(S[e['id']],key=lambda s:s['position']): print('  STEP',s['label'],'|',s['body'])
      for x in sorted(N[e['id']],key=lambda x:x['letter']): print('  NOTE',x['letter'],'|',x['body'])

# Principle model picks: Principle-Strengthen and Principle-Conform

69	                part = bd[1].split("### Choice Notes", 1)[0]
70	                for m in re.finditer(r"^\*\*(.+?):\*\* (.+?)(?=\n\n\*\*|\Z)", part.strip(), re.S | re.M):
71	                    steps.append((m.group(1).strip(), m.group(2).strip()))
72	            cn = sec.split("### Choice Notes", 1)[1] if "### Choice Notes" in sec else ""
73	            for m in re.finditer(r"^\*\*\(([A-E])\) (.+?)\.\*\* (.+?)(?=\n\n\*\*\(|\Z)", cn.strip(), re.S | re.M):
74	                notes[m.group(1)] = (m.group(2).strip(), m.group(3).strip())

21-## PT142-S1-Q1 | Most Strongly Supported | C
22-
23-> Children should be discouraged from reading Jones’s books. Reading them is like eating candy, which provides intense, short-term sensory stimulation but leaves one poorly nourished and dulls one’s taste for better fare. In other words, the problem with letting children read Jones’s books is that _______.
24->
25-> Which one of the following most logically completes the argument above?
26->
27-> (A) it will lead them to develop a taste for candy and sweets
28-> (B) too many children may become frustrated by their difficulty and stop reading altogether
29-> (C) their doing so interferes with the development of appreciation for more challenging literature
30-> (D) their message may undermine the positive teaching done by parents
31-> (E) children may become so enthralled with books that they will want to spend all their time reading
32-
33-### Argument Breakdown
34-
35-**Statements:** Children shouldn't read Jones's books. Reading them is like eating candy: a strong, quick thrill that leaves you poorly nourished and dulls your taste for better food.
36-
37-**Evaluate:** The blank starts "In other words", so it restates the analogy in plain terms. Map each piece: candy = Jones's books, poor nourishment = not much to gain, a dulled taste for better fare = less appetite for better books.
38-
39-**Goal:** Reading Jones dulls children's taste for better books.
40-
41-### Choice Notes
42-
43-**(A) Out of Scope.** The candy is a comparison. Nobody says the books make children want actual sweets.
44-
45-**(B) Opposite.** Candy is easy and pleasant. The analogy suggests the books are too easy, not too hard.
46-
--

430->
431-> Which one of the following judgments most closely conforms to the company policy?
432->
433-> (A) Mac is a full-time employee who has been with the company for exactly one year. He just adopted a child, and he applied for parental leave three months ago, so Mac should be granted the leave.
434-> (B) Sara has worked full-time for the company for three years, and she applied for parental leave four months before the recent birth of her child. However, she should not be granted the leave because she took a leave of absence within the past year.

[external_agent_tool_result]
> (D) Dudley has worked part-time for the company for the past seven years. He applied for parental leave over three months before the birth of his child, so it should be granted to him.
> (E) Maria has worked full-time for the company for the past two years and applied for parental leave six months prior to her adoption of a child, so she should be granted parental leave.

### Argument Breakdown

**Rule:** An eligible employee gets up to six weeks of paid leave. Eligible means applying at least three months before the leave starts *and* having at least one year of full-time employment when applying.

**Evaluate:** Check each person against both requirements, measured at the time of application. Also watch for reasons to deny that the policy never gives.

**Goal:** Someone who meets both requirements and is granted leave.

### Choice Notes

**(A) Violates Rule.** Mac has one year now, but he applied three months ago, when he had only nine months. He wasn't eligible when he applied.

**(B) Violates Rule.** Sara meets both requirements. A recent leave of absence isn't a reason the policy recognizes.

**(C) Violates Rule.** The policy grants "up to" six weeks, so wanting only two is fine. Denying Hal on that basis doesn't follow the policy.

**(D) Violates Rule.** Dudley has worked part-time. The policy requires full-time.

**(E) Correct.** Maria has two years full-time and applied six months ahead. She meets both requirements, so she gets the leave.

# Read-only REST pull of the LR question + explanation tables. Run from the vault root.
# Usage: python system/scripts/lsatlab-pull/db_pull.py [out_dir] [table ...]  (default: data/db next to this file; the four LR explanation tables)
import sys
from pathlib import Path
sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]))
from project_paths import project_path, VAULT_ROOT
import sys
import json, re, urllib.request, os
env={}
for f in [VAULT_ROOT / '.env', project_path('lsatu', '.env')]:
  for l in open(f,encoding='utf-8'):
    m=re.match(r'([A-Z_]+)=(.*)',l.strip())
    if m: env[m[1]]=m[2].strip('"')
U=env['VITE_SUPABASE_URL'];K=env['VITE_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY']
def req(p,body=None,tok=None):
  r=urllib.request.Request(U+p,data=json.dumps(body).encode() if body else None,headers={'apikey':[REDACTED],'Content-Type':'application/json','Authorization':[REDACTED] or K)})
  return json.load(urllib.request.urlopen(r))
tok=req('/auth/v1/token?grant_type=password',{'email':env['LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL'],'password':[REDACTED]})['access_token']
out=sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv)>1 else os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)),'data','db')
os.makedirs(out,exist_ok=True)
for t in (sys.argv[2:] or ['lr_questions','lr_explanations','lr_explanation_steps','lr_explanation_choice_notes']):
  rows=[];o=0
  while True:
    b=req(f'/rest/v1/{t}?select=*&limit=1000&offset={o}',tok=tok);rows+=b;o+=1000
    if len(b)<1000:break
  json.dump(rows,open(os.path.join(out,t+'.json'),'w',encoding='utf-8'),ensure_ascii=False)
  print(t,len(rows), sorted(rows[0].keys()) if rows else '')

  answerChoices?: Partial<Record<'A' | 'B' | 'C' | 'D' | 'E', unknown>>;

const sharedPassageMetadataPrefixPattern =
  /^\s*(?:The following passage is used for Section\s+\d+,\s+questions\s+\d+\s+through\s+\d+\.|Questions?\s+\d+\s+(?:through|and|[-–])\s+\d+\s+refer to the following passage\.)\s*/i;
const sharedPassageRangePattern =
  /^\s*(?:The following passage is used for Section\s+\d+,\s+questions\s+(\d+)\s+through\s+(\d+)\.|Questions?\s+(\d+)\s+(?:through|and|[-–])\s+(\d+)\s+refer to the following passage\.)\s*/i;

function stripSharedPassageMetadataPrefix(text: string): string {
  return text.replace(sharedPassageMetadataPrefixPattern, '').trim();
}

function normalizeProseHardWraps(text: string): string {
  return text
    .replace(/\r\n?/g, '\n')
    .split(/\n{2,}/)
    .map((paragraph) => paragraph.replace(/[ \t]*\n[ \t]*/g, ' ').replace(/[ \t]{2,}/g, ' ').trim())
    .filter(Boolean)
    .join('\n\n');
}

function getSharedPassageRange(text: string): { start: number; end: number } | null {
  const match = text.match(sharedPassageRangePattern);
  if (!match) return null;

  const start = Number(match[1] ?? match[3]);
  const end = Number(match[2] ?? match[4]);
  if (!Number.isFinite(start) || !Number.isFinite(end) || start > end) return null;

  return { start, end };
}

const dialogueIntroPattern = /^[A-Z][A-Za-z’' -]{1,80}:\s+/;
const dialogueTransitionPattern = /[.!?]["”')\]]?\s+[A-Z][A-Za-z’' -]{1,80}:\s+/;
// Captures: 1) sentence-ending punctuation, 2) the candidate speaker label
// (without its trailing colon/space), 3) the colon + whitespace that follows.
const dialogueTurnBreakPattern =
  /([.!?]["”')\]]?)\s+([A-Z][A-Za-z’' -]{1,80})(:\s+)/g;

function normalizeDialogueSpeakerBreaks(text: string): string {
  if (!dialogueIntroPattern.test(text) || !dialogueTransitionPattern.test(text)) return text;

  return text.replace(dialogueTurnBreakPattern, (match, punct, label, colon) =>
    looksLikeSpeakerLabel(label) ? `${punct}\n\n${label}${colon}` : match,
  );
}

export function normalizeStimulusText(text: string): string {
  const sharedRange = getSharedPassageRange(text);
  let normalized = stripSharedPassageMetadataPrefix(text);

  if (sharedRange) {
    normalized = normalized
      .replace(new RegExp(`\\s+${sharedRange.start}\\.$`), '')
      .trim();
